| locations/breakwalls-jetties-riprap.md | a boat can sit safely as close as 5 ft off the rock, even where it ends abruptly, as long as wind isn't pushing the boat in | single-source | `bydQzE3F9yM` | verified verbatim ("you could be five feet away and be in safe water... as long as the wind's not blowing in there") but corroborated nowhere else in this note's citations |
| locations/breakwalls-jetties-riprap.md | night sessions at "that breakwater" produced teen-lb calico bass vs. very few over ~4 lb caught there by day | single-source | `RUuPNpK0x-s` | verified verbatim ("teen jumbos caught on that breakwater at night and I don't know a very many over four that have been caught during the day") but the specific weight figures are corroborated nowhere else in this note's citations |
| locations/breakwalls-jetties-riprap.md | the panelist runs a 5.5 in bait at night on the federal breakwater | single-source | `ZoIzpyyS9xw` | verified verbatim ("about half, that's five-five inch, it really works great on that with federal breakwater") but the 5.5 in figure is corroborated nowhere else in this note's citations |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the fog paragraph — a Cedros-based captain running the island's yellowtail grounds in fog 5–6 months of the year, navigating on radar alone | contradicted-by-source | `d0yGBQDeY_4` | the quote is real but the speaker is not a Cedros captain and the water is not Cedros: it is a Simrad sponsor testimonial dropped into the episode, and it names its own ground — "the run out to the Gulf of Mexico from **Venice marina** is from seven to thirty miles long, about five to six months out of the year we are running in fog... twenty-five miles later when I'm sitting in the **Gulf of Mexico** it's still gonna be working". The same ad runs verbatim in `3yK3JYrKoZY`, which is how it was caught. Nothing places the fog months, the zero visibility or the radar/sonar division of labour on the island's grounds, and `planning/electronics-and-sounder.md` is linked off it — a region-separation question under CLAUDE.md's labelled-contrast rule, not just a cite question |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the surface-iron band of **60–68 ft** over rock that eats jigs, carried in the Lead, the bathymetry table and forward onto locations/north-point.md | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | `IdMJnlg_rZk` states no depth anywhere. 68 ft is `2cxeg_AE418` ("this is what we come cedral for boys holy 68 ft water") and 60 ft plus the jig-eating rock are `_MygUHw19q8` ("they're fishing 60 ft of water so you had to pull hard we lost about easy 10 jigs cuz they straight rocked us"). `_MygUHw19q8` also gives "how deep is it J 7 80 7 74" and "hey man 73 70" in the same water, so 60–68 is narrower than the record supports. All three videos are the same trip and all three sit in `sources`, so this is cite precision rather than fabrication — but the band is the zone's headline parameter and it propagates to the child spot pages |
| locations/cedros-island.md | San Benito squid and the yellowtail on them sit at **30–40 fathoms**, feeding right off the bottom — in the infobox `depth_band`, the bathymetry table and How it fishes | contradicted-by-source | `JaKSGkZ6CAc` | the captain says "they're all feeding on squid **30 40 50** Fathoms deep here... so why we're getting them right off the bottom" — 50 fathoms is dropped; he separately gives "they're out here in 30 35 [fathoms] of water here". Already ledgered against `species/yellowtail.md` for the same figure from the same cite; recorded here because this page is where it originates and `locations/san-benito-island.md` carries it forward in its own `depth_band` and Lead. Feeding-off-the-bottom is verbatim |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the San Benito bottom is jagged rock at about **200 ft**, and sinkers and iron must come up off it immediately after touchdown | contradicted-by-source | `ilINTeknKB4` | both statements are verbatim but neither is at the Benitos: they belong to the lingcod/rockfish drift the boat makes **after** leaving them — "got our Yellow Tail done and out of the way... we're stopping along the coast to let them do some link cod fishing before we head back out and do some tuna fishing", teased in part 2 as "one of Kevin's secret spots for some Ling Cod and Rockfish". The same passage says "fill up the spool since we're going to be going deep to about 300 ft" moments before the 200 ft call. The What's there rockfish/lingcod row and `locations/san-benito-island.md`'s `depth_band` both carry the mis-placement forward |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the named kelp bed "off the Cedros/Gono islands" fished at anchor with bombed casts allowed to fall | contradicted-internal | `NGxyOlPx3ug` | `NGxyOlPx3ug` never mentions Cedros. Its ground is Dono / Donal Island ("we're going to start at Dono", "let me fill you in on Donal Island", "at Dono Island") — the stop this same page's Spots section puts 112 mi up-coast and rules explicitly **out** of the zone under "Not in this zone". So the page both excludes Dono and fishes it. The technique is verbatim ("Aaron put us right in the middle of the kelp dropped anchor", "just throwing as far as I can and letting it fall and they're eating it on the fall"); only the place is in dispute. `species/calico-bass.md` already carries a separate flag on this same video |
| locations/cedros-island.md | grouper (cabrilla) and black seabass are the reason for the **80 lb** just-in-case line class on a Cedros trip | contradicted-by-source | `ldVj0BoB-kE`, `WE643Fue1_A` | `WE643Fue1_A` names a different pair — "lastly some 80 pound just in case you never know if we want to do some **black sea bass** fishing even some bigger **white sea bass** fishing 80 pound might be handy". Grouper/cabrilla is never mentioned anywhere in that video and white seabass is dropped. This page's own line-class bullet in Doctrine & conflicts states it correctly ("black seabass and bigger white seabass"), so the What's there row contradicts the body of the same note |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the 2024 fly-in trip ran **30–45 lb** yellowtail with the biggest weighed at **45 lb**; a separate trip ran a lighter **19–33 lb** | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk`, `9kGpcEHqIUc` | two defects in one row. `IdMJnlg_rZk` weighs nothing — both figures are `_MygUHw19q8` ("they're big fish from 30 to **45 50**"; "this is the biggest one that came in today weighing in at... 45 lbs") — and `2cxeg_AE418` weighed its own day's biggest at **35.5 lb**, which the row drops. And `9kGpcEHqIUc` is not a separate trip: it is a 55 s clip of the `_C7TJoGT0nw` interview carrying the identical exchange ("About 29 lb personally. Yeah, all of our fish down there were honestly 19 to I think the biggest in our group was 33 lb"), same speaker, same trip — as this page's own evidence file already records |
| locations/cedros-island.md | Cedros calico grade runs **3–6 lb** with 7 lb fish called by eye | contradicted-by-source | `_r_qKX_7080`, `yjwIGFzWO8I` | neither video states 3–6 lb. `_r_qKX_7080` calls "they're four Pounders at least", "a little four pounder", "nice three pounder" and one "that's 7 or eight lb" fish — and the 7 is the captain's call, not the angler's ("Captain just said that's 7 lbs we're not going to weigh him"), with bigger fish only hearsay ("the monsters that we've been hearing about seven eights or nines"). `yjwIGFzWO8I` says only "go catch some five and six pound calicos" and makes no by-eye 7 lb call at all. The published range is a splice of two videos and is stated by neither |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the mid-day calico quote "it's raining calicos out here" | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | the quote is `2cxeg_AE418` — "that's a lot of Calico look at all those Calico it's raining Raining calicos out here". `IdMJnlg_rZk` carries the mid-day programme and the 5.5 in mini-iron ("it finally slowed down so we headed in to catch some Calico bass... small righteous 5 1/2 in") but not the quote. Already ledgered against `species/calico-bass.md`, which flags the same fused Day-1/Day-2 claim; recorded here because the zone page carries the same fusion under a single id |
| locations/cedros-island.md | midday wind pushes crews off the morning grounds onto a calico point | contradicted-by-source | `ILBl12Jm7-0`, `I-QBxuV2p7M` | the co-cite holds for `ILBl12Jm7-0` only ("it's about midday and the wind has picked up big time... we're hitting this last Point... for these calicos"). In `I-QBxuV2p7M` the causation runs the other way: a dead bite drove the move to calicos ("this is that time of the day where you haven't gotten a bite... we're deciding going to N[ativi]dad to go fish calicos for an hour before this wind picks up") and the wind then turned them back from the calico ground ("the wind uh cut us down halfway there so we had to turn around"). One cite states the rule; the other states a different, partly inverted sequence |
| locations/cedros-island.md | a Cedros crew lost roughly **10 jigs in two days** to fish that rocked them | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | the line is `_MygUHw19q8` ("we lost about easy 10 jigs cuz they straight rocked us and we were lock down drags") and the transcript scopes it to no period at all — it is said in a day-3-plus-day-4 wrap. "In two days" is the KB's. Numerically precise and load-bearing: it is the page's evidence that the zone taxes terminal tackle |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the morning-bite paragraph — a 43 lb fish on another boat around 07:00–08:00 as they pulled up, off the dock at 05:30, about five fish hanging by 06:30 | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | none of it is in `IdMJnlg_rZk`; it is `_MygUHw19q8`, and it is two different mornings. Day 3 opens "it is 5:30 boys... starting a little earlier today... we saw them come up early yesterday around like 700 maybe 800 as soon as we pulled up the other boat just went out and they already **had** like a 43 going" — they arrived on a fish already hooked rather than watching the hookup — and the "five hanging by like 6:30" is the Day-4 open looking back at Day 3. Three clock times and a weight, all precise, all under the wrong id of the same trip |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the three-way mix of surface iron, yo-yo iron and dropper loop "running through the day at the San Benitos" | contradicted-by-source | `SH7zOA9ZF3o` | `SH7zOA9ZF3o`'s wrap-up names a different three — "they were biting on the **bait**, surface iron and the yo-yo jig" — and contains no dropper loop anywhere. The `u0scEBby7nA` trio at Cedros ("surface iron dropper Loop yo-yo I mean you name it they're B[iting] it") is verbatim, so the two grounds are not running the same mix and the note's "same three-way mix" joins them. Matters because the next sentence then adds bait as a fourth thing fishing "alongside them" |
| locations/cedros-island.md | a **1/2 oz** sliding sinker with a nose-hooked sardine for suspended fish | contradicted-by-source | `ilINTeknKB4`, `d0yGBQDeY_4` | `d0yGBQDeY_4` gives the 1/2 oz sliding sinker ("try something different little sliding sinker egg 1/2 ounce took off with it") but never a nose-hooked sardine — the only bait named on the hook there is a mackerel — and never a suspended-fish rationale. Both come from `ilINTeknKB4` alone ("when the fish were suspended a half o sliding sink Sinker NOS hook sardine did the trick"). Its sister episode from the same trip, `97clKtVsEOs`, specs the same rig at **1 oz** ("the Sardine live sardine on uh an ounce sliding sinker"), so the corpus carries two weights for one rig and the page publishes one |
| locations/cedros-island.md | working seabirds over a wide-open bite as the stated trigger for reaching for a jointed three-piece hard bait | contradicted-by-source | `fnaGBGbrhqU`, `9kGpcEHqIUc` | "seabirds" is a caption artifact for **Cedros**. The same sentence from the same interview is rendered "when you hear about **seedros**, you hear about a wide open yellowtail bite" (`_C7TJoGT0nw`) and "like how you hear about **Cedros** being" (`9kGpcEHqIUc`); only `fnaGBGbrhqU` reads "seabirds", and no bird behaviour, bird sign or working birds appear anywhere in any of the three. All three are cuts of one audio, so the double cite is one source. The lure half stands; the trigger half is a transcription artifact carried as doctrine, and it links `planning/search-and-glassing.md` off it |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the Doctrine drag bullet — locked drag with "these are too big for all that noise, just use the leverage", against "keep that loose drag" | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk`, `RPSRH0jwyw4` | both quoted positions are misplaced. The lock-the-drag doctrine is `_MygUHw19q8` ("lock in your drag... we were lock down drags and you have to pull as hard as you can"), not `IdMJnlg_rZk`, and "I'm going back down these are too big for all that noise just use the leverage" is spoken at a different moment mid-fight, about rod leverage during head shakes, never in connection with a drag setting — the bullet welds it to the locked-drag position. In `RPSRH0jwyw4`, "keep that loose drag dude it's all about the drama" is coached over a **halibut** ("possible hal[ibu]t is not fighting like a Yellow Tail"), which also bears on the bullet's closing claim that the corpus records no crew fishing loose from the cast |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the lodge amenity list — massage and jacuzzi in a premium package, laundry, and a **15-person dock crew** that handles all gear | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | three errors. The **15** is the airstrip team, not a dock crew — "immediately after getting off the plane... he probably has a team of 15 guys waiting to take all your luggage all your rods" — and the dock crew is described separately with no number ("they wouldn't let you touch a thing all their team was on it"). The **jacuzzi** did not exist at filming: "there is massages included in the premium package and they have a Jacuzzi **coming**... still coming still in the works" (`_MygUHw19q8`), a hedge the note hardens into an available amenity. And laundry is `2cxeg_AE418`, massage/jacuzzi `_MygUHw19q8` — only the open bar and the tomahawk steak are in the cited video |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the Coronados contrast — the same crew's sessions ran **10–12 lb** schoolies that "hit it like a freight train" | contradicted-by-source | `4pT0efBD1j0` | the compound joins two different fish. "There you go they're like sick what like tan 10 12 pounder right now" is one fish at 02:48; "look at this big holy moly... you hit it like a freight train insane" is at 04:46 over a visibly bigger one. The transcript never states a session grade (the day's total is "three on the boat one in the jig two on the bait") and never attaches the freight-train strike to the 10–12 lb class. Load-bearing: the whole grade-and-behaviour contrast, closing with "it is why a Coronados retrieve and hookset habit costs fish here", rests on the join. The chum-then-cast programme is verbatim |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the long-range leg described as "one 8-day charter" that fished 170 mi south of the border on day one then ran a further ~150 mi to San Benito | contradicted-by-source | `JaKSGkZ6CAc` | it is a **five**-day: "wrapping up our very first day which would be Thursday on our 5 day", "Beautiful weather all five days here", "another successful 5-day Charter on board the Top Gun 0[80]". The 8-day charter in this corpus is the Intrepid (`97clKtVsEOs`, `ilINTeknKB4`), and `locations/san-benito-island.md` splits the two correctly, so the zone page is the outlier. The same transcript also carries a second, much shorter figure for the same run from the captain on the water — "we got about uh what do we got 75 miles to go here" — against the voice-over's 150 mi; both mileages are otherwise verbatim |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the Dono and Sacramento Reef stops sit roughly 112 mi up-coast of Chester's Rock, **off San Martin** | unverifiable | `_r_qKX_7080` | San Martin is never named anywhere in the transcript, and Sacramento Reef appears once, garbled and unlocated ("it's having your own private Dock and Sack Reef it's crazy"). The 112 mi separation is verbatim ("then we're going to go another 112 miles"). This sentence is what excludes Dono from the zone — and the page simultaneously carries a Dono kelp-bed observation inside its own Structure section under `NGxyOlPx3ug`, so the placement decides two things at once and rests on a claim the source does not make |
| locations/cedros-island.md | metered bait at Cedros in **183 ft** | unverifiable | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | the line is `2cxeg_AE418`, not `IdMJnlg_rZk`, and the caption gives a bare number with **no unit** — "yell 183 and we have a lot of ba below us at the bottom". The "ft" is the KB's. The figure appears twice on this page (the Forage paragraph and the bathymetry table) and is the deepest sounding attributed to Cedros itself |
| locations/cedros-island.md | an angler's Cedros baggage allowance of **35–40 lb** combined between tackle and clothes on the charter flight | unverifiable | `_C7TJoGT0nw` | same unseparated caption digit string as the take-home figure already ledgered for this page — "allowed 3540 lbs between your tackle and everything like that. Tackle clothes." — so "35–40 lb" is a reading, not a stated range. It is the more defensible of the two readings, since the 28 lb bag is called "in that nice sweet spot" immediately below it and that only makes sense against a 35–40 ceiling; but it is also the anchor the take-home figure is then justified against, so the two cannot corroborate each other |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the take-home fish total, flagged inline as resting on a digit string "that both cuts of the interview render identically" | unverifiable | `_C7TJoGT0nw`, `iHwvUl0dhxk` | companion row to the standing take-home flag: the two cuts do **not** render it identically. `_C7TJoGT0nw` reads "I was able to take about **354** lbs of fish home" and `iHwvUl0dhxk` reads "about **3540** lbs of fish home" — the caption is unstable across two uploads of one audio, so neither figure is confirmed. Nor are they independent: `iHwvUl0dhxk`, `9kGpcEHqIUc` and `fnaGBGbrhqU` are all short clips excerpted from the single `_C7TJoGT0nw` interview, so every claim on this page citing two or more of them has one source. The 20 lb halibut and the calico-bycatch framing are verbatim in both |
| locations/cedros-island.md | Cedros calico bass are fished on the beach at about **30 ft**, tight to structure | contradicted-by-source | `_C8w6zeVPak` | the condition is inverted. The speaker contrasts the prior year against the trip being filmed — "here last year **complete opposite** the fish were nice and tight it seems like every day we've been finding the fish out just a little bit deeper **not** um about 30 ft deep close to be right and tight". Tight-to-structure is the year before; this trip found them deeper and not tight. The 30 ft figure and the beach setting are real, and the caption is garbled enough that the row deserves a flag either way |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the fly-in aircraft is "genuinely tight and with **no restroom** — use one before boarding" | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | the transcript says only "make sure you guys use the restroom right before"; it never states the aircraft lacks one. A hedge hardened into a fact. The 12-people-possibly-16 count in the same list item is verbatim ("it fits exactly I want to say 12 people might be it might be 16 but it is tight in there") |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the Cedros voluntary 100% catch-and-release agreement for calico bass, grouper (cabrilla) and black seabass, on the summer-spawning-aggregation rationale | single-source | `ldVj0BoB-kE`, `Zo92MG459gQ` | the two cites are one source: `Zo92MG459gQ` closes with the same Jose interview that `ldVj0BoB-kE` is cut from, and both renderings match word for word ("all the local companies of cedos Island decided to catch and release every single Calico bus grouper and Black Sea bus"; "by the hundred and lately there's have been none or very few"). "Cabrilla" is the KB's gloss — the speaker says only "grouper". The channel's own video title calls it "a regulation change", which the interview itself contradicts ("it's our fishery we're responsible for them and no one else is going to tell us what to do"), so the note's operator-agreement reading is the right one. `I-QBxuV2p7M` (2022) shows retention at Cedros ("we're keeping some and we're letting some go"), dating the agreement to 2023 rather than earlier. Propagated verbatim to five child spot pages — **ext-verify**: whether the Cedros operators' voluntary 100% catch-and-release for calico bass, grouper and black seabass is still in force as of 2026, and whether any Mexican federal or state rule has since been layered on top of it |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the rock-cutoff pattern "runs across the whole triangle" | contradicted-by-source | `_r_qKX_7080` | the transcript describes rock cutoffs only at Chester's Rock — "he took me obviously right into the the Rocks", "these are some mean fish down here in these rocks", "we're in shallow water we lost a few guys using too light a line" — and never generalises them to the triangle. A zone-observed behaviour scoped past its source, which is what the style guide's scope-every-claim rule exists to catch. The San Benitos half of the same sentence is verbatim in `SH7zOA9ZF3o` |
| locations/cedros-island.md | the "puff of current" bite that ran to 40 lb, placed at the Rose Island / Dondo Ridge lee the crew ran to after being blown off Chester's Rock | contradicted-by-source | `_r_qKX_7080` | the bite is narrated in the past tense inside the Chester's Rock wrap-up — "we saw a few come up we got a little puff of current and uh we got a nice little hit on the yellows we had them up to I'd say 40 Pounders" — and only then "so we're going to start here with Dondo Ridge and kind of work our way up". It belongs to the rock they were blown off, not to the lee they ran to. The wind/lee fallback itself is verbatim ("we're in the Lee of the island and that was key we kind of got blown out of Chesters"), and both place names remain asr-uncertain and are not clearly the same place. Carried forward on `locations/chester-s-rock.md` |
| locations/cedros-island.md | "macro banks" is a bank that sits offshore of the island, giving a mixed yellowtail-and-dorado bite | contradicted-by-source | `yjwIGFzWO8I` | the transcript never places the bank offshore. Its one positional line runs the other way — "today we're going to the **mainland** and chester's rock to fish some calicos after we do some morning yellowtail fishing here" — which also puts Chester's Rock on the mainland, against `_r_qKX_7080` placing it "right off of south of sedus Island". The name is one garbled mention followed immediately by bait-making ("just waking up here on the macrobanks and all the boys are making mackerel here"), so "macro banks" may itself be a mishearing, as the page already caveats. The mixed yellowtail-and-dorado bite is solid |
| locations/cedros-island.md | a cluster of this page's direct quotes and doctrine sits under `IdMJnlg_rZk`, the Day-1 video of a three-video trip, rather than the sibling videos that carry them | contradicted-by-source | `IdMJnlg_rZk` | aggregate row for the residue not itemised above. "Don't look at the videos and think ah it's easy no the opportunities are there you still have to make the cast you have to be ready" and the chase-and-back-down instruction ("chase him chase him chase him back him down... too big going into the Rocks") are `_MygUHw19q8`; the jig-shape exchange ("try flat oh yeah I gave it up yeah let me try a flat flat might work") and the bait-barge/bait-boat and micro-red-crab forage detail are `2cxeg_AE418`; the departure-day 05:20 / 08:00–08:30 / 10:30 clock and the "20 minutes" to the big-fish grounds are `_MygUHw19q8`, though the How it fishes paragraph cites all three jointly and only the Spots bullet carries "20 minutes" under `IdMJnlg_rZk` alone. `IdMJnlg_rZk` genuinely carries the travel chain, the arrival half-day, the mid-day calico programme, the 17:40 school and "they're not like slamming like freight train it's like slow". The jig exchange is also unresolved on camera — one speaker calls the jig too small, a second answers "No it should work", and only the flat is confirmed tried, so "went bigger, and switched to a flat profile" reports a sequence the transcript does not show |
| locations/island-structure.md | for a land-attached perpendicular ridge, straight-into-the-beach current is "marginal" while straight-off-the-beach is "dead" | contradicted-by-source | `6TBxHnkYXI0` | the transcript does not grade these two cases separately for this structure type — its worked reef example says into-the-island current means "that pinch point's not there" and off-the-island current "that's the same exact thing," i.e. equally unproductive, not a marginal-vs-dead split. The graded language may derive from a different (kelp-bed) example in the same transcript, but is not stated for the ridge/reef case it is attached to here |
| locations/island-structure.md | Catalina's ~500-fathom curve: the uphill current compresses climbing the slope, spins eddies, and aggregates bait and kelp paddies along the curve | single-source | `HnqiE05vdXs` | verified nearly verbatim ("we noticed that we were sitting on the edge of the 500 fathom curve"; "that's traveling at a good speed gets condensed and goes faster and that faster movement creates these eddies which in turn will aggregate not only bait along this fathom curve but also kelps") but the ~500-fathom figure and the compression-creates-eddies mechanism rest on this one transcript only, and the same source is the only citation for the identical claim in `conditions/current-structure.md` — no independent corroboration in the KB — **ext-verify**: whether Catalina's fathom curve is charted at ~500 fathoms and whether current compression against a rising slope is the correct oceanographic mechanism for eddy formation there |
| locations/island-structure.md | the current climbing Catalina's fathom curve is the "uphill (summer counter)" current | contradicted-by-source | `HnqiE05vdXs` | the transcript calls this current only "going uphill" / "running offshore or uphill" and never uses "counter" or "summer counter"; that current-type label is attested elsewhere in the KB (`conditions/current-structure.md`, "summer uphill (counter)current") but from a different source, `h3PTupup17I`, not cited here |
| locations/island-structure.md | paddies and bait found on Catalina's fathom curve are worth working, unlike the same sign in open water between features | contradicted-by-source | `HnqiE05vdXs` | the transcript describes the kelp paddies aggregating along this curve as generally unproductive on their own — "you see a million kelp, they don't usually have fish on them" — treating the bait/current relationship, not the paddies themselves, as the productive signal |
| locations/island-structure.md | parallel current over a land-attached reef opens a pinch-point upwelling that turns the whole current-exposed expanse into huntable ground for calico bass | contradicted-by-source | `6TBxHnkYXI0` | the source gives these as two separate examples: a kelp-bed/calico-bass example where parallel current makes "that entire expanse... huntable ground" with no upwelling mentioned, and a distinct San Clemente 90-ft-flat reef example where a pinch point creates an upwelling that aggregates bait for "game fish" generally (not calico bass specifically); this note's row fuses the two into a single mechanism attributed to calico bass |
| locations/island-structure.md | yellowtail key on bait aggregated by pinch-point upwellings and fathom-curve eddies | contradicted-by-source | `6TBxHnkYXI0` | "fathom-curve eddies" does not appear anywhere in this transcript (that concept belongs to the separately-cited `HnqiE05vdXs` worked example elsewhere in the same note) and the pinch-point-upwelling passage in this transcript names "game fish" generally, not yellowtail specifically |

| locations/coronado-islands.md | the chain is "roughly 13 nm off the San Diego bay entrance" (lead, Getting there, and front-matter `distance_nm: ~13 nm`) | contradicted-internal | cameron, `D5DR7Kx42_A` | the directly-linked `species/yellowtail-coronado-islands.md` states the same run as "roughly 13 mi", verbatim from its cited transcript ("approximately 13 miles from San Diego"); 13 nm ≈ 15.0 statute mi, so two linked pages carry different real-world distances under matching "13" figures and different attributions (cameron vs. video). Spot-library geometry favours the nautical reading: Zuniga Jetty (32 40.300 / 117 13.400) to Pukey Point, the chain's northernmost charted mark (32 26.850 / 117 18.000), is 14.0 nm — 16.1 statute mi — so 13 statute mi falls short of any charted Coronados mark while 13 nm is within 1 nm of the near end. Both figures cite cameron, and the cameron source (`sources/spot-lists.md`) carries the position only — it states no distance, so neither number is stated by a source and both are derived. Paired with the 13 nm / 13 mi row already open on `locations/coronado-islands.md`; flagged, not reconciled |
| locations/pukey-point.md | the ground east of Pukey Point "fishes as rockfish rather than as part of the island circuit" | contradicted-by-source | cameron | the cited library entry (`sources/spot-lists.md`) is named "East of Pukey Point (**yellowtail** & rockfish)" and lists yellowtail first, so the source does not support restricting the ground to rockfish. What the library does do is file it under the "Rockfish areas (Coronados vicinity)" heading rather than under "Coronado Islands", which supports the second half of the claim (not part of the island circuit) but not the first. `locations/coronado-islands.md` carries the same rockfish-only framing ("East of Pukey Point — the rockfish ground north of the chain"), so the drift is on both pages; `locations/east-of-pukey-point.md` keeps the library's full name and states no species |
| locations/middle-grounds.md | the Coronados chain is "a chain the corpus gives as roughly 13 nm out as a whole" | contradicted-by-source | `D5DR7Kx42_A` | the only corpus statement of the chain's distance is "an island chain approximately 13 miles from San Diego" (`D5DR7Kx42_A` @00:00:18) - statute miles, not nautical (13 mi = 11.3 nm). This page attributes the nm reading to "the corpus"; `locations/coronado-islands.md` states the same 13 as nm sourced to cameron and already carries a `contradicted-internal` flag for the mi/nm split, so this row is that conflict propagating to a second page. Charted geometry backs the nm reading (nearest Coronados waypoint, East of Pukey Point, is 13.3 nm from Zuniga Jetty; the chain's near end cannot be 11.3 nm out), so the number stands and only the unit attribution is wrong |
| locations/middle-grounds.md | the Coronados troll is "prospecting that happens to hook fish rather than the presentation itself" | contradicted-by-source | `HTowqnwAMeA` | the transcript supports "prospecting" verbatim - "trolling is an excellent way of prospecting a zone quickly and effectively" - but contradicts the second half: the very next lines say the fish "will be keyed in to a specific presentation, a specific depth or a specific vibration that just gets them going," then give color-by-light selection doctrine ("overcast... super high viz colors... super bright... darker colors") and a full trolling rig spec (6-7 ft rod, Torque/Fathom 40, 80 lb braid, 15-20 ft top shot of 50-60 lb). The source treats presentation as load-bearing, not incidental. Note also that this speaker's "slow troll" means live bait and is framed as a top PRODUCING method, not prospecting - only the artificials troll is the search tool |
| locations/middle-grounds.md | the `HTowqnwAMeA` cite used as Middle Grounds / Coronados trolling doctrine | contradicted-by-source | `HTowqnwAMeA` | neither "Coronado" nor "Middle Grounds" appears anywhere in the transcript; its geography is generic ("local yellowtail," "Summertime fishing in SoCal"), and its one geographic specific points away from this bank - "especially when you're looking at these local zones like above the border, they've seen a lot of slow troll baits in their lives" - i.e. US water, while the Middle Grounds is Mexican water by this page's own Getting-there section. The cite carries general SoCal yellowtail-trolling doctrine and is not evidence about this mark |
| locations/middle-grounds.md | "a fish boiling within range of a blind-cast iron" (evidence file: "blind-casting surface iron when a yellowtail boiled about 10 ft off the boat and ate the cast") | contradicted-by-source | `4pT0efBD1j0` | the eat was not on the blind cast. Verbatim @00:07:16-00:07:26: "so this fish has decided to bust up in front of us doing blind casts and legit a fish literally pops up like 10 feet from the boat, cast it again got his ass" - he was blind casting, a fish busted ~10 ft off, and a follow-up SIGHTED cast at the boil got bit, which is the crew's own stated preference ("unless we see them boiling and we could cast on them then that's gonna be our go-to"). The "Middle Grounds" name, the birds ("still chasing some birds down right now middle grounds") and "it's about like 4 p.m." are all verbatim and stand. Flagged on both the note and its evidence file |
| locations/evidence/middle-grounds.md | the 4 p.m. Middle Grounds fish is identified as "a yellowtail" | contradicted-by-source | `4pT0efBD1j0` | the transcript names no species in the Middle Grounds segment or on the landed fish (@00:07:27-00:08:08 ends only "that's on the dw1, damn weight special by jri"); the same day also produced "three good sized calicos" and a bluefin at south island, so the fish is not pinned to yellowtail. The trip premise and wrap do mention yellowtail, so the inference is reasonable - it is just not stated. Same line: "surface iron" is a class inference from the on-camera gear (JRI DW1 jig, Trinidad 20, 40 lb, 106 CUI stick), never said on camera and the DW1's type (surface vs. yo-yo) is never given; and 2020-08-18 is the manifest UPLOAD date - the transcript's only time anchors are same-day narration ("about 1 pm right now," "it is sunset time," "pick them up tomorrow morning") |
| locations/desperation-reef.md | the source that anchored here "calls the ground the 'West End tuna zone'", set against the spot library charting Desperation Reef at the southeast end | contradicted-by-source | `Rb5I2ljAqeE` | the transcript names the spot exactly as the library does - "this is called desperation reef is what it's called" (@00:11:45, immediately after "are we on a bank here... we're anchored up are we on a high spot") - and never utters "West End tuna zone". "West End" appears once, as one boundary of that season's tuna mass: "the whole tuna Mass is from actually the West End all the way down to uh 10 miles off point lman" (@00:11:34); the zone label the crew does use is "the blue pin Zone" (bluefin zone, @00:01:38). So the name discrepancy this page carries as unreconciled may not exist in the source at all - the anchoring and squid-on-the-bank reads it sits beside are both verbatim ("the reason these fish are here right now is cuz they're squid up on the bank", @00:12:05). The same framing is carried on `locations/san-clemente-island-back-side.md` (as a flagged gap) and in the `Rb5I2ljAqeE` evidence line on `techniques/flyline.md`, so this is one reading propagated to three notes |
| locations/desperation-reef.md | flylined dead squid drew repeat bites at 40-90 lb, best "probably close to 90"; baits under a balloon worked the 30-40 lb grade the same session | contradicted-by-source | `Rb5I2ljAqeE` | both grades exist in the transcript but neither is attached to the presentation the note attaches it to. 40-90 lb is the intro's trip-wide bluefin grade ("the quality of blue fin tuna that decided to join the party 40 to 90 Pounders that absolutely brawled", @00:00:20); the squid bites themselves name no size and no species ("hot squid on squid bobar that's the third bite on squid", @00:05:08; "that one on squid", @00:05:27). "probably close to 90 right there" (@00:06:22) is verbatim and is the trip's biggest bluefin. 30-40 lb comes from a session-grade line that lists it beside the yellowtail ("great fish 18 to 25 lb yellow 30 to 40 lb tuna", @00:13:49), not from any balloon segment - balloons appear separately (@00:14:11, @00:21:13) with no grade. A third figure sits in the same trip: "nice 30 say 40 or 50 lb tuna that came to the boat" (@00:17:56). The 18-25 lb yellowtail and the sardine flyline are verbatim and stand |
| locations/desperation-reef.md | with current running against the wind the crew added a half-ounce weight "to keep the bait swinging naturally instead of letting the current straighten it out" | contradicted-by-source | `Rb5I2ljAqeE` | the condition and the weight are verbatim - "it's uh currents Against the Wind here we're swinging like a fat Out of Hell / guys not 2 oz half oce weight" (@00:03:28, captions; "fat" is ASR for "bat") - but the rationale is not in the transcript and reads inverted: the swinging is the complaint, not the goal ("swinging hard like that snapping it", @00:03:51), and no no-weight rule is invoked on camera. `techniques/flyline.md` carries the identical rationale in its `Rb5I2ljAqeE` evidence block, so the reading propagates to both notes. Whether the half ounce was meant to damp the swing or preserve it is what a re-watch settles |
| locations/desperation-reef.md | zone-wide the fishery keys to squid presence, not the calendar | contradicted-by-source | `YZtX1MiT0y8` | the 2022-04-21 bight report states no calendar-versus-conditions principle. Squid appears twice, only as the bait in use in two named areas ("they're getting fish up there on squid", @00:02:38; "on the back side east end they're fishing squid", @00:03:13); Catalina, the Coronados, San Diego and the offshore bluefin sections make no squid reference at all, and the report's own zone-wide framing is wind and water temperature ("the big concern was all this wind would really mess things up", @00:01:06). The doctrine may well be right and is stated elsewhere in the KB for yellowtail - it is this cite that does not carry it |
| locations/desperation-reef.md | a squid bite going quiet is a relocation problem rather than an absence problem, "so follow the squid on the meter before writing the stop off" | contradicted-by-source | `YZtX1MiT0y8` | the first half is verbatim - "it look it looks to me like a squid zone bite so maybe that squid moved out into deeper water maybe they just need to relocate that... i'm sure they'll relocate those fish it's not like they left" (@00:03:37) - but the mechanism the note adds is not: the source's remedy is a weather window and boats searching ("get some good weather over there for a few days get those boats looking around again"), and the transcript makes no meter or sonar reference in connection with squid (its only sonar mention is "of sonar schools", @00:06:46, about offshore bluefin). | locations/desperation-reef.md | a 2016-11-29 trip ending about six miles off the island outside Desperation Point in 1,200-1,300 ft | contradicted-by-source | `AIHvJj-paoo` | the geography is verbatim - "we just made a jump went from the 209 to out San clenia area" (@00:02:36, ASR for San Clemente) and "we're actually just about 6 miles off the island outside of desperation point I think we're at about 12 1300 ft of water" (@00:02:40) - but 2016-11-29 is the manifest UPLOAD date, not a stated trip date, and the narration twice places the fishing in October ("come late October the tuna had settled in right out in front of SoCal", @00:00:06; "the fish are still biting in the afternoon on a late October on the West Coast", @00:20:26). `locations/209-312.md` carries the same six-miles/1,200-1,300 ft record without a date. | locations/14-mile-bank.md | swordfish "named in the San Diego offshore-bank rotation — the Ridge, the 9 Mile Bank, the 14, the 43, the 178 and the 302 — all worked on a preferred down-and-in drift along the contour edges" | contradicted-by-source | `yMiBtZ7k8-w` | the six-mark list merges two speakers and two unrelated passages. "The 14" appears once, in Ali Hussainy's generic life-on-the-surface example four minutes later — "if you roll out to your spot whether that's the 9 mile bank or the 14 or the 43 and you got whales and dolphins and birds" (@00:54:26) — with no drift, depth, season or date attached. So this bank's only swordfish claim rests on a passing "your spot" illustration, not on a named rotation. The 150–350 fathom band the row checks against IS verbatim in the same source ("whether it's in 150 fathoms out to you know 350 fathoms", @00:29:31) and stands. Flagged in two places on the page (the yellowfin row and the "wind is what ends the day" paragraph) plus the evidence file; one finding |

The 152 and Avalon Bank are dropped, which is what makes the loop a loop rather than a straight inshore leg, and a 60–80 lb bluefin spot "at the end of the shipping lanes" sits between Avalon Bank and this mark (@00:06:49) — worth keeping distinct, since that grade is easy to misattribute to this bank. The page's conclusion (the bank was the day's inshore end, not a first stop) is unaffected; the 29.3 nm / 28.3 nm distances check out against the spot library |

Both parses use the same three numbers; the split between them changes whether the bank reads warmer or cooler than the water just off it, which is the differential this page's "read the bank against the water just off it" doctrine turns on. Every other figure on the page reproduces exactly (91 3.1, 300 4.0, Corner 7.8, 140 10.2, 166 12.4, 51 12.6, 182 13.6, 181 15.4, 284 16.2, 289 17.8, 302 23.8, 178 24.7, Pyramid Head 21.5, Widow Rock 31.2, Point Loma Pipe 34.9), so this is one omission, not a bad method |

Load-bearing on a spot page with charted coordinates: it is the reason the page routes bluefin rather than rockfish — **ext-verify**: whether 32°39.333'N 117°58.417'W sits inside a current CDFW/federal rockfish closed area, and the current groundfish depth limit and season at that position |
| locations/us-waters.md | a California fishing licence is "checked before you ever reach the water — a sportboat crew asks for it at the gangway and an angler without one does not board" | contradicted-by-source | `LsFMBCa9DOQ` | the on-camera licence check is real ("your California fishing license there sir" … "welcome aboard"), but three details are added on top of it: the transcript never says gangway, never states a carry-on-your-person requirement, and no angler is turned away. The no-licence moment is garbled banter — "I don't have one no not yet they a a't getting on the boat they let you on the boat they let Vegas on the boat" — which reads as a joke about someone who WAS let aboard, not a refusal. The page's evidence file states it harder still ("told on camera he is not getting on the boat") and carries its own row |
| locations/us-waters.md | "Buy it ahead of the season rather than on the dock" — the licence-timing doctrine | contradicted-by-source | `E9YtH56Dngo` | the source's entire licence content is "another big thing not to … forget to stock up on for this year is your fishing license by far the best christmas present you can give anybody". | locations/us-waters.md | the GO ID is a 10-digit number printed one line below the barcode on the California fishing licence | contradicted-by-source | `Qs9oEsh3b_w` | the digit count is verbatim; the position is off by one line and points the reader at the wrong number. The same wrong wording is carried on species/california-spiny-lobster.md and in this note's evidence file, so a fix is a cluster-pass edit, not a single-note one. Separately, `b19_AJjYCok` requires name AND GO ID on a hoop-net buoy while `Qs9oEsh3b_w` (2020, later) describes GO ID only — **ext-verify**: the current CDFW licence layout (which line below the barcode carries the GO ID) and whether a buoy must carry the angler's name in addition to the GO ID |
| locations/us-waters.md | "private boats have been boarded and cited for fishing the wrong side of" a rockfish closure line | contradicted-by-source | `iAr6sbRC384` | the source's whole account is "the only reason I found that out because some of my buddies that run farther boats and stuff got boarded" (ASR-garbled; "farther" reads as *charter*) — boats his buddies RUN, which reads as charter/commercial passenger vessels rather than private boats, and that account mentions no ticket, fine or citation. The rest of the paragraph verifies: the line runs at varying depth ("inside that line some of it's 800 feet") and the chart-app fix is his ("download it on your phone … punch in the numbers and it'll make a li[ne] for you"). The evidence file repeats the same "private boats … boarded and cited" reading and carries its own row |
| locations/us-waters.md | "A jig fished in US waters runs two hooks — a single assist on the nose and a single on the tail", flagged on the page as having no corpus source | contradicted-by-source | `iAr6sbRC384`, `shZCjX2-fkI` | the cap is NOT uncited — `iAr6sbRC384` states it, and states it narrower than "US waters": in a California rockfish seminar, as a rockfish gear limit, "remember it's a to [two] hook with in there so don't get in trouble … if I were to put one of these on top … they considered three hooks so be careful" (ASR-garbled, @00:18:39–00:18:54). That source gives no issuing authority, no statewide scope, and no nose-plus-tail configuration — the configuration is the KB's own. Pulling the other way, `shZCjX2-fkI` (San Diego bluefin, 2021-01-14) describes four-hook chandelier rigs as routine — "where you have two hooks hanging off the top two hooks down below or a single big j hook down below it all works" — with no limit mentioned. The page's standing gap flag ("no corpus source: the issuing authority and as-of date") therefore understates what the corpus has and overstates the rule's scope — **ext-verify**: the actual CDFW hook-count rule, whether it is statewide or groundfish/rockfish-specific, and its current as-of date |
| locations/us-waters.md | where the two-hook cap bites, and whether the fleet observes it | contradicted-internal | `iAr6sbRC384` | three notes state the same cap three ways. This page and sources/regulatory-claims.md say an assist pair on the front alone "reaches"/"maxes" the limit; techniques/slow-pitch-jigging.md says the same pair "already puts you over the limit". A two-hook pair is either legal or it is not, and the two readings disagree on exactly that. Meanwhile lures/knife-jigs.md — which this page links as the cap's home — documents several THREE-hook builds fished successfully in the San Diego fleet (a 6/0 double on top plus a single 6/0 below on a Rich Win 270 g; a factory Fish Lab 250 g pre-rig of two 6/0 up top plus a 9/0 slider), which the cap as written would make illegal in US water. Cameron to settle the rule before the wording is reconciled |
| locations/us-waters.md | the Southern California Bight runs "from the Santa Barbara Channel to the border" | contradicted-internal | — | the child region page this sentence introduces defines its own extent differently: locations/socal-bight.md opens "The Bight runs from **Point Conception** to the US border", and places the Santa Barbara Channel only as the home of its northernmost zone (12 Mile Reef). Point Conception is the headland at the Bight's northwest corner; the channel is the water inside it. The jurisdiction rung should summarise the region rung rather than redefine its northern anchor — a one-word fix, but it is the child page's definition, so it is a transform edit rather than a fact-check one |
| locations/evidence/us-waters.md | "an angler without one is told on camera he is not getting on the boat" | contradicted-by-source | `LsFMBCa9DOQ` | no such refusal happens. "Gangway" is also not in the transcript. Companion to the locations/us-waters.md row on the same claim |
| locations/evidence/us-waters.md | "the presenter recounts private boats being boarded and cited over the rockfish closure line" | contradicted-by-source | `iAr6sbRC384` | the account is "some of my buddies that run farther boats and stuff got boarded" (@00:22:45–00:22:52; ASR-garbled, "farther" reads as *charter*) — boats his buddies run, not private boats — and it mentions no citation, ticket or fine. The Navionics recommendation and the position-over-depth framing in the same evidence line do verify. Companion to the locations/us-waters.md row on the same claim |
| locations/socal-bight.md | Nine Mile Bank and "the 14" are "the exceptions" to fathom naming, sitting 9 and 14 **statute** miles off the coast | contradicted-by-source | `Rf1HKJG-SDg` | two additions on top of a verbatim naming convention. The source treats the two as examples, not a closed pair: "there's banks like that up and down the coast but most of them are the banks are called after the highest part of the bank" (@00:06:04). And it never says statute — "the nine mile bank that's not nine miles deep that's nine miles off the coast… 14 is 14 miles off the beach" (@00:05:29), miles unqualified. The unit is load-bearing (9 nm vs 9 statute mi = 1.4 nm of search box) and is the same ambiguity already flagged on locations/middle-grounds.md and locations/14-mile-bank.md. The fathom convention and the 182 / 209 / 43 figures verify verbatim (@00:02:00–00:04:14). The Access and range section restates the two-bank count, and locations/zone-lexicon.md carries "statute" too, so a wording fix is a cluster-pass edit |
| locations/socal-bight.md | the best paddy water is south of San Clemente Island / Catalina and Dana Point to Oceanside southward; paddies thin out and hold fewer fish north of Dana Point | contradicted-by-source | `HzE4FHHcvTk` | the source gives three coequal hedged options rather than one best water — "your best bet is i would say for that type of action is south of san clemente island or south of catalina or along the beach below dana point um towards the ocean side" (@00:03:36) — and does not index the thinning to Dana Point: "that fishing continues up in the u.s waters basically all the way to san clemente island and heck for that matter all the way up the coast uh to get on the inside of cat. when you get further up the patties are fewer and further between and they're not always holding" (@00:03:20). "Hold fewer fish" hardens "not always holding", and the driver the source actually gives is boat pressure ("the closer the patties are to a dock the more likely they are to get hit"), not geography. conditions/kelp-paddies.md, locations/dana-point.md ("the region's northern paddy limit") and locations/oceanside-north-county.md carry the same reading |
| locations/socal-bight.md | a strong NW event rolls surface temperature down 5–8 °F at the northern Channel Islands and the LA/Ventura shelf | contradicted-by-source | `YZtX1MiT0y8` | the transcript's only degree figure is a hedged single-site reading of Long Beach harbour taken off a friend's video — "it looked like it was like 56 degree water which is i think seven maybe eight degrees off from where it was before this wind along the beach" (@00:05:15). Not 5–8, not measured at the northern islands, not regional; what the source states for the islands is an absolute ("they're in the low 50s", @00:01:14). The source also never names the wind NW ("it's been blown outside all week"). locations/bight-geography.md, which owns the wind-rollover map, states the same 5–8 °F under a standing cite-unresolved + adjudicated flag against `HzE4FHHcvTk` — Cameron ruled 2026-08-26 that **the map stands** and is to be backed by BightSST rollover statistics rather than re-sourced, so this row questions this page's CITATION, not the map. This page states the figure plainly against a third id, and conditions/water-temperature.md, linked as support, carries no NW-rollover content at all. The zones that held verify verbatim |
| locations/socal-bight.md | the fall quote "conditions become pretty important… look 100 % at your temp chart and chlorophyll" | contradicted-by-source | `XLVUhV8DW64`, `OYOda6T3f-8` | the sentence exists in neither source as quoted. `OYOda6T3f-8` carries the two halves about four minutes apart — "conditions become pretty important when looking offshore finding that right water and everything else" (@00:02:28) and "if I were going out I would like 100 on your temp chart and your chlorophyll when looking for fish" (@00:06:28, ASR of "lean 100%") — and the ellipsis fuses them into one utterance. `XLVUhV8DW64` contains neither fragment; its nearest wording is "make some educated guesses using your chart using your SST using your chlorophyll" (@00:07:10). A direct quote in a seasonal-regime table is exactly where fusion should not happen |

The antecedent is unspecified "fish" in a passage covering dorado, bluefin and marlin; the only sized-tuna line is elsewhere and generic ("most of the bigger tuners still being caught at night or by the four-pack guys", @00:04:56). seasonal/september.md and species/bluefin-tuna.md carry the same bluefin reading on the same cite, so the fix is a cluster-pass edit. "Northern corner" for Santa Barbara Island is likewise a KB inference from a north-to-south enumeration |
| locations/socal-bight.md | a compact spot reads in 20–30 minutes and a complex is a half-day commitment; a compact spot is worked by idling up, grading the meter and committing or leaving | unverifiable | `Blh2BA-7Ono` | no corpus source gives a time budget for either box type. The cited source says only "you should expect to spend quite a bit of time driving around don't just expect to drive a spot and find those fish" (@00:08:50), and the compact-spot contrast it does make is bare ("it's not like running to the 302 or 371 or even running up to uh the stuff off the west end of cat", @00:08:38) — no procedure, no minutes. locations/zone-lexicon.md, named here as the figures' home, states them uncited as well, so the numbers have no origin anywhere in the KB. This matters because the sentence is the justification for the page's largest gap flag - the fifty-one uncharacterised bank positions |
| locations/baja-pacific-north.md | the Ensenada offshore grounds lie 20-50 mi west | contradicted-by-source | `0bcDBGzQnGw` | the range fuses two different positions. The crew's stated plan is "head West out maybe 20 to 30 miles straight west" (11:19); the 45-50 mi figure is where the conference FLEET was sitting, which the crew deliberately fished away from - "I know the fleet is about 45 50 miles from where at their lamp here if we could find fish away from them that's our goal" (11:36), with no direction given for the fleet. A third figure, "about 35 miles from Ensenada" (20:49), carries no bearing. No 20-50 mi ground is stated |
| locations/baja-pacific-north.md | one Ensenada tournament day totalled roughly 180 mi run | contradicted-by-source | `U1AgwmlY5bI` | 180 mi is the running total mid-day, not the day's total: "it's uh one of those days we uh probably drove 180 mil today we went North we went South... total miles on the boat so far we still got another 40 to get in" (17:04). The day lands at roughly 220 mi. The figure is load-bearing here because it is the page's only stated Ensenada day-range number |
| locations/baja-pacific-north.md | the Cedros fly-in chain - CBX, walking a rod bundle across the pedestrian bridge, a ~2-hour charter flight, a 10 ft jig stick down the aisle | contradicted-by-source | `xIUKmH9ccgQ` | the paragraph carries one cite, `xIUKmH9ccgQ`, and that transcript supports only its last sentence. It describes a bus route: "we're going to go to San [Y]sidro where a bus is going to pick us up. Just across the border in TJ is where we get our Mexican permits... we go to [E]nsenada where we... board our plane" (00:30), with the aircraft given as "a small eight passenger plane or 12 passenger plane" and no duration. It contains no CBX, no bridge, no flight time and no rod length. All three CBX-route details are verbatim in `IdMJnlg_rZk`: "we got to cbx which is in TJ... we bundled up our rods bundled up all of our gear and we walked it straight across the border and as soon as we uh crossed the bridge we had one of Toto's guys waiting for us" (00:23), "you just have to hold on for 2 hours" (01:32), and "you can bring a 10t rod you see that that's 10 ft right there" (01:48). `IdMJnlg_rZk` IS in this note's `sources`, so this is a cite-placement error rather than a missing source |
| locations/baja-pacific-north.md | Regulatory - Mexico. Jurisdiction: CONAPESCA for fishing, INM for entry | unverifiable | - (no cite) | no source cited on this page names either agency, and the identical attribution on locations/mexican-waters.md is already ledgered there as a name its two cited transcripts never give. The as-of stamp, the CDFW-does-not-apply note and the verify-current instruction all follow the content rules correctly; it is only the agency naming that is uncorroborated in the corpus. Because it is a regulatory claim, CLAUDE.md requires a web check regardless of corpus support. **ext-verify**: whether CONAPESCA is the correct fishing-licence authority and INM the correct entry authority for a recreational angler in Baja California, current as of the check date |
| locations/evidence/baja-pacific-north.md | `ilINTeknKB4` logged at "San Benito Island" for the 200 ft jagged-rock drift | contradicted-by-source | `ilINTeknKB4` | the evidence one-liner repeats the placement error flagged on the main note, and it is the entry the note's claim rests on. The drift is a lingcod/rockfish stop along the coast on the run north, not at the San Benitos: "got our Yellow Tail done and out of the way... we're stopping along the coast to let them do some link cod fishing before we head back out and do some tuna fishing" (06:22), then "okay gang going to set up make a drift looking at 200 ft of water real Jagged lot of rocks down there so you got to be careful with your sinkers and your iron you hit the bottom get them up off the bottom real quick or else you'll lose them" (07:47). The depth, the "jagged" characterisation and the get-them-up-quick instruction are verbatim; only the place label is wrong. Companion to the locations/baja-pacific-north.md row on the same cite |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | the corpus reaches this region partly "on charters out of Cabo San Lucas" | contradicted-by-source | `ll7r4A6atno` | `ll7r4A6atno` is not a charter - the presenter fishes his own boat with his wife ("we're fishing in cabo kelly and i are going out to try to catch some sierras today"; "I untie the boat and it starts blowing"). The charter route into Cabo is `dgauGbNxP84` ("this is edwin's charter and we're just out here with edwin today") and the two Hurricane Fishing days (`XH-Hrfet6To`, `Mwx5AAXNMvE`, "hurricane fishing offers affordable charters"). The three-ways framing is right; only the cite behind the charter leg is wrong |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | the region's banks lie "450-488 mi" south of San Diego | contradicted-internal | `_r08B4bItAY` | the band excludes one of this page's own grounds: locations/lower-banks.md, a child of this page, puts the lower banks roughly 608 nm south of San Diego on an 11-day trip, "well beyond Alijos Rocks and the Ridge" - outside both the mileage band and the "six, seven and eight days out of San Diego" line in Access & range. `_r08B4bItAY` also states 488 mi and 450-460 mi as two separate figures for two grounds, never as a range, and never says "south of San Diego" (the departure port is implied only) |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | Alijos Rocks is "fished as a dedicated stop on the fleet's six-, seven- and eight-day trips" | contradicted-by-source | `tjBeR9tWd4s` | the transcript frames the two grounds as where the boats START the season, not as an itinerary stop: "A lot of the long range boats, including the Searcher, they're going to start their 6 7 8 day trips here. The trip lengths and both place names verify verbatim; "dedicated stop" and "out of San Diego" are note-side - the only geographic anchor spoken is "Carl with Fisherman's Landing and team Searcher" |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | the "lower banks" near Magdalena Bay ran giant yellowfin at 150 to 300-plus lb | contradicted-by-source | `UuyqTE21-kc` | the only "150 to 300" in the transcript is the cold-open negation about a different ground, spoken on the run home: "traveling north we come across one of Baja Mexico's best fishing grounds the ridge there might not be 150 to 300 lb y[ellowfi]n tuna cruising around but there is definitely some awesome fishing for yellowtail Dorado" (00:16). Three defects - the ground is the Ridge, not the lower banks; the figure is a negation, not a grade; and there is no "plus". The transcript's actual lower-banks grade is "the giant Tunas of 130s to 350 lb" (22:35), and `ASitOLYzFEA` lists individual fish (350, 338, 325, 311, 310, 292, 280, "9 over 140") rather than a 150-300 band |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | three different giant-yellowfin grades for the lower banks across the KB, two of them on this page from one cite | contradicted-internal | `UuyqTE21-kc`, `ASitOLYzFEA` | this page says 150 to 300-plus lb in The fishery and 130 to 350 lb in the Season shape row, both cited `UuyqTE21-kc`; species/yellowfin-tuna.md says 140-350 lb off `ASitOLYzFEA`; locations/lower-banks.md carries all three ("130s to 350 lb", "150 to 300-plus lb", "roughly 140 lb up to 350 lb"). | locations/baja-pacific-south.md | "A yellowtail came off the Ridge yo-yoing at 180 ft, 10 ft off the bottom" | contradicted-by-source | `3tQ1_xiqwVU` | the transcript attaches no place to the drop - the boat is "fishing down here in front of Mag Bay" when the call is made ("A little bit deeper. Yellow tail, I think"). The depth pair is verbatim; the place, the species and the landing/loss all come from joining two moments |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | the 180 ft yellowtail placed on the long-range Ridge / Uncle Sam Bank | contradicted-internal | `3tQ1_xiqwVU` | locations/ridge-uncle-sam-bank.md explicitly says the 180 ft, 10 ft-off-the-bottom fish "is carried on the Magdalena Bay page, not here", and locations/the-ridge.md - Magdalena Bay's own Ridge, a different bank reached out of the boca - carries it. This page states it in the Alijos-Rocks-and-the-Ridge paragraph, and uses the single name "the Ridge" for both banks (the Modesto Main and Thetis stubs below are the Mag Bay one; the 450-460 mi Uncle Sam Bank rows are the long-range one) |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | a mangrove interior at 12-15 ft "whose snook program works down to 2 ft tight to oyster-covered roots" | contradicted-by-source | `EcQK5elTPJE` | both figures are Dave Hansen's general mangrove fishing, not a snook program: "the fishing in 12 to 15 ft of water is blind boggly" and "when you're fishing in the inside Lopez M in the mangr I fish 65 lb braid and an 80 lb floral carbon leader and I'm fishing in 2 ft of water because if you look at the mangrove Roots they're all oysters". | locations/baja-pacific-south.md | "one presenter's own best day there was 72 marlin, with the boat capable of 'over a hundred' the same day" | contradicted-by-source | `gimFbgN5Jjk` | the line is "My best day was 72 marlin. | locations/baja-pacific-south.md | jigs to roughly 80 ft at La Bocana drew a hookup "every single drop" | contradicted-by-source | `qri15R3caYE` | "every single drop" is not the transcript's wording - the captions render the two jig drops "every single draw" and "every single saw" ("about 80 feet down here every single saw made again just like that"). | locations/baja-pacific-south.md | the Winter row's dated instances - January 2021 Cabo, December 2014 Cabo, February 2018 La Bocana | unverifiable | `ll7r4A6atno`, `dgauGbNxP84`, `Mwx5AAXNMvE`, `qri15R3caYE`, `Cobp85UvHmM` | none of these five videos states a month or year in its content; every date is the upload stamp. "La Bocana was fished in **February 2018**" and "a December 2014 Cabo charter" read as fishing dates, while this same table's Spring row expressly separates the two ("what exists carries upload dates rather than fishing dates"). Using upload dates is legal under the absolute-dates rule; asserting them as fishing dates on one row while distinguishing them on another is the inconsistency |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | Gray whales arrive at Magdalena Bay "about mid-January" | contradicted-by-source | `EcQK5elTPJE` | the transcript never identifies the species: "the wh[ales] show up in about I think about mid January they['re] there now" - the word "gray" appears nowhere - and the timing carries the speaker's own hedge, dropped here. The dating is title/metadata only, and the "09/10" title is itself ambiguous. The evidence file states this correctly ("resolved to September 2010 from companion uploads", "per the title"); the main note hardens it to "on camera" |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | Capt. Mike Tahr's second-week-of-November-2019 wahoo trip "closed that season with 'tremendous catches'" | contradicted-by-source | `4uNPLknRAQg` | the quoted phrase belongs to the general-months statement, not the trip: "I think fall fall is the best. The trip is described separately as "last year in the fall, uh Captain Mike Tauter had an awesome trip. Um and those were on kelp patties", with "the last trip of 2019 that Captain Mike ran that had all those wahoo were the was the second week in November". Second week of November 2019 and the season-closing framing verify; the quote does not, the kelp-paddy detail is dropped, and the captions render the surname both "Tahr" (00:36) and "Tauter" (03:23) |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | giant yellowfin of 130 to 350 lb move into the Magdalena-Bay-to-Cabo lower banks every fall to breed and spawn | single-source | `UuyqTE21-kc` | verified verbatim ("every fall the the giant Tunas of 130s to 350 lb Tunas make their way up into that mag Bay lower Banks area to breed and spawn", 22:32) but it is one narration line on an unregistered channel and no other corpus source states a spawning migration for this ground. It is load-bearing - it is the stated reason the lower banks fish in fall at all - and it is a spawning-biology claim with published literature outside the corpus - **ext-verify**: whether Pacific yellowfin spawn on the Baja Pacific banks between Magdalena Bay and Cabo San Lucas in fall, and at what grade |
| locations/baja-pacific-south.md | "A **November 25** stop on the 'lower banks' anchored on one giant-tuna school for four days" | contradicted-by-source | `ASitOLYzFEA` | November 25 is the dock date, not the stop date: the host opens at the landing with the boat inbound - "we're down here going to cover uh one of the Intrepid trips that just got back, they're coming in within the hour, it's uh November 25th, a day before Thanksgiving" (00:10) - days after the four-day stop. The four days on the anchor verify four separate times ("sitting there for 4 days anchor", "four days on the anchor in the same spot", "one school for 4 days you didn't move - we never moved", plus "a hook on the anchor rope"), and "lower Banks" is the crew's own term. The dropped "day before Thanksgiving" qualifier also constrains the year the row calls unresolved |

"You want to start with at least 8 oz and go to minimum of 16 or if not heavier" is the general what-to-bring sinker range. The slider itself is "an 8 oz slider. Sometimes you put two or three of these things on there," and 16-20 oz belongs to the TORPEDO alternative on "a short breakaway, maybe like 6 in". Nothing in the source is called a 16 oz standard |

<!-- review:ledger:end -->

## Standing — flagged in the note, no action required

The claim stands and the ⚠ flag stays on the note. Each row says what
rule decides it and what evidence would change it.

| note | claim | category | cite | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| techniques/wind-in-your-face-positioning.md | rock-cod/dropper-loop drift is "the standard format on nine times out of 10 rockfish boats" | contradicted-by-source | `S9sQ8Vg8-5o` | the transcript attaches "nine times out of 10" (restated earlier as "a good 90% of the time") to drifting vs. anchoring — "nine times out of 10... going out on these Rockfish boats we are typically drifting and not on anchor" — not to the dropper-loop rig/format being standard; the rig itself is separately called only "one of the most simple and basic rigs... probably one of the most productive," with no figure attached. Flagged in both the main note and its evidence file. **Stands**: flag, never delete; a wording fix distinguishing the drift-frequency stat from the rig-productivity claim is a transform-pass edit |
| techniques/night-bass-fishing.md | "even 50 lb is light for a calico that can reach 10 lb fished around structure" | contradicted-by-source | `XyKF34C5iB4` | the source frames 50 lb fluoro as the common/preferred leader for a 10 lb-class calico around structure, used for abrasion resistance, not as insufficient for stopping power: "people like 50 lb really for calos fish that gets to 10 lbs. But... when you're fishing around structure, whether that's rocks or kelp or boilers, you're really using that just for abrasion." The 15-20 lb light-line comparison in the same passage checks out. **Stands**: flag, never delete; a wording fix is a transform-pass edit |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | bluefin run sub-30 lb schoolies to 300 lb+ cows in the same season off SoCal and northern Baja, grade unpredictable trip to trip | contradicted-by-source | `PrdPJy26H8c` | the cited transcript carries no numbers at all: its whole bluefin content is "unless we're going blue fin fishing cuz you never know what size blue fin you're going to run into", said about downsizing a lure box. The weight span is real in the corpus — `shZCjX2-fkI` ("schools of 40 to 60 pounders… a chance at a two to three hundred pound bluefin"), `5to3Q5P7w90` ("30 to 150 pound class fish"), and the note's own mid-December row for the sub-30 lb floor — but this cite does not carry it, and it sits in the note's lead where a reader takes it as the sourced grade envelope. **Stands**: flag, never delete; re-citing is a transform-pass edit |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | Tanner/Cortez in November–December is night-dominant with 300 lb fish through November | contradicted-internal | `Z3rZqy2Pi8E` | seasonal/november-december.md, the note this row links, states 60–80 lb fish with a few 100+ lb mixed in on that same source id, and this ledger's Resolved section records that the 300 lb figure there was legacy uncited text corrected in the 2026-08-28 transform. The species router still carries the number the seasonal note dropped, so the KB states two grades for the same window and the same source. Cameron to confirm the router follows the seasonal note; the fix is a transform edit, not a fact-check one |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | schools sighted near San Martin Island in January 2021 in 63–65 °F water | contradicted-by-source | `shZCjX2-fkI` | two unlinked statements ~11 minutes apart have been joined. The sighting: "probably 10 days ago or something like that the guys on long trips just outside of san martin island there had giant schools of big bluefin tuna that **didn't bite all that well** but stuck with the boat". The temperature, separately and second-hand: "i saw some information the other day that the water was 63 to 65 degrees where they were catching the fish" — no place attached. It matters because 63 °F is the floor of the note's front-matter `sst_band_f` |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | Tanner Bank produced an anchored bluefin bite in mid-August 2024, months before the fall condensation window | contradicted-by-source | `ntQXxcH5sjI` | the anchored bite on camera is a YELLOWTAIL bite — "sit and anchor on day one was intense and the boat did not stop getting bit", fish called "slug yellowtail", "nice full load of yellowtail". Bluefin appear only on day two, and slowly: "on day two the fishing slowed down with a few fish yellowtail and blue fin landed here and there." The place and date check out ("about 90 to 100 mi west of Long Beach… we anchor at about 120 ft of water and we were fishing in mid August", upload 2024-08-29). The claim is load-bearing — it is the note's counter-example to its own seasonal slide |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | a bank 42–43 mi off Ensenada held confirmed breezing and kite-raised fish across an August 2024 tournament while ~40 boats landed 4 fish on day one and 7 on day two | contradicted-by-source | `Ix0gG0-l3v0` | the bank is never named and the distance is a guess — "how far did we just travel uh we just went uh **I think** about 42 43 miles". The word breezing is never used (the sighting is "they're up on top"), and the only kite result is a refusal: "we had a swirl under it just take commit", with the crew's own summary "in the end we did not land any fish during the tournament." The counts are right but hedged, from two speakers ("I'm guessing… like 40 boats", "three boats caught fish", "maybe four pieces and today uh seven pieces"). Presence ≠ catchability is the point the passage makes, so the overstated sign weakens its own argument |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | front-matter `sst_band_f: 63-76` as the species temperature band | contradicted-internal | `5to3Q5P7w90`, `shZCjX2-fkI` | the band is built from the two outliers the note's own prose calls soft evidence. conditions/water-temperature.md: the 76 °F foaming reading is "above the usual ceiling… it softens the case **without moving the productive band**"; seasonal/september.md says the same in the same words. The 63–65 °F end is a January presence reading, not a fishing band. The machine field is what a day plan reads, so it publishes as doctrine what three notes agree is not doctrine. Cameron to set the band the field should carry |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | bluefin swim into the dirtiest, coldest water available to be on bait | contradicted-by-source | `HWx1jDTGsng` | the source states it as one of two cases, not a rule: "they'll go into the dirtiest coldest water **some days** … and then **other days** you find them right where you expect nice blue water", and its baseline is the opposite emphasis — "typically this bluefin is gonna like the cooler side of the water", "temperature breaks are always good". The chalky-transition-water half of the paragraph IS verbatim and stands. Single-source ≠ wrong; the finding is the dropped conditional |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | red crab is the forage the STANDARD flat-fall colour is chosen to imitate | contradicted-by-source | `RbqOKkINSCM` | the red-crab imitation is verbatim ("this imitates the color of the red crabs"), but Capt. Dave Hansen frames it as his personal contrarian pick AGAINST the prevailing colour: "a lot of lures are colored for fishermen they catch fishermen… everyone says glow in the dark works the best you know why because that's what they're using but i like to use natural colors that's me", and he would switch to glow if glow were getting bit. `fgTmUq78ofQ` independently names glow, not red crab, as the night default — so "standard" inverts the corpus. The forage fact stands; the word standard is the finding |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | bait squid staged on a bank, named as the reason a bluefin bite held on one high spot | contradicted-by-source | `Rb5I2ljAqeE` | the causal line is there — "the reason these fish are here right now is cuz they're squid up on the bank" (Desperation Reef) — but "these fish" follows rockfish and yellowtail talk ("we got yellowtail last trip right here"), and bluefin is inferred from the wider trip, where bluefin were eating squid baits. Listing squid under bluefin's DOCUMENTED forage states a species the source leaves open. Sourcing a bluefin-specific squid line closes it |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | one Cortez-era trip had fish gorging on microbait day and night, with a small-profile tungsten jig the only thing reaching them at weight | contradicted-by-source | `CsKEtrcLgiQ` | the quote is verbatim ("I was aboard the Independence and those fish were gorging on microbait day and night") but no location is named anywhere in the transcript — "Cortez-era" is not in the source. And the stated reason is profile, not reach: "I probably wouldn't have gotten that fish cuz it was just what they were gorging on", with the speaker de-generalizing — "it's definitely not something that you need on every trip." The note's own evidence file states this correctly; the router's phrasing drifted |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | the one dependable rule is that bluefin break every rule, so the search anchors on bait rather than on a temperature or a spot | contradicted-by-source | `HWx1jDTGsng` | the source rejects water COLOUR as the anchor, not temperature: "the one thing that these bluefin what you can count on is [they'll] break every rule… the number one thing that I always want finds a bait yeah **clean water dirty water whatever**". In the same interview he calls temperature breaks "always good" and starts every plan from water temperature and clarity. Bait-first stands; "rather than on a temperature" is the note's addition and it contradicts the same speaker |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | the same bite unpredictability described from the anchor — a pick that hangs two, three, four fish all day, then nothing | contradicted-by-source | `3T4c3Zez_DM` | no anchor job is stated: the scene is a morning big-bluefin bite at San Clemente Island on the 2017 Top Gun 80 five-day, and the only nearby audio is the garbled "can you get one of them deck on the ankle too". The source also hedges both ends the note hardens — "sometimes they want to cooperate all day where you get a pick and there two or three or four hanging all day long **or at least for a while** and then **sometimes** they shut off" |
| species/bluefin-tuna.md | night fish ~20 mi off San Diego at 240–360 ft | contradicted-by-source | `eehDVb6_GoI` | the transcript says "it's **20 to 30 miles** from san diego"; the note narrows the range to its near end. The depth band and the 250 g jig floor are exact and stand ("anywhere from 240 to 360 feet deep… so you want a lure that is 250 grams or greater"; "if you're trying with 150 or 120 gram lure you're not going to get down to that depth"). A run-distance figure is a trip-planning input, which is why a narrowed range is worth a row |

Magdalena Bay is a large mangrove system with many channels, and nothing establishes whether this water is the same as the Lopez Mateos mangrove channels a different crew fishes on the zone page — if they are one water the KB splits one ground in two, and if they are not, the zone's tide doctrine is being read across two different estuaries. The gazetteer published the page because the depth band (25–30 ft), the mangrove-edge standoff (50–100 m) and the three presentations are all specific and cited, and because the channel is the middle rung between the mangrove interior and the boca (2026-08-30 gazetteer) — **stands**: flag, never delete. A charted position, a name from a local source, or a second crew describing the same channel settles it |
