| locations/san-felipe.md, locations/sea-of-cortez.md, locations/cortez-north.md | whether the San Felipe mothership trip is datable — [Sea of Cortez](../locations/sea-of-cortez.md) states it "dated only to 2022… with no season stated" and [Cortez North](../locations/cortez-north.md) flags that "nothing in the corpus dates its year" at this end of the region | contradicted-internal | `dEPuDrhoClM`, `0dNfsle54Wo` | the companion tackle-bag video says outright "why we were actually out there in November of 2022" (`dEPuDrhoClM` 00:00:37) and the last episode signs off "Tony Reyes 2022" (`0dNfsle54Wo` 00:20:03), so the whole nine-episode program is a **November 2022** trip. | locations/lopez-mateos-magdalena-bay-boca.md, species/snook.md | whether the boca at Magdalena Bay is rock or open bottom | contradicted-internal | `EcQK5elTPJE` | one source supports both readings and no transcript describes the bottom directly. The break-off side: a single-speed reel means "breaking off all day" against "the structure at this mark", and a hooked grouper "heads straight back for the rock" and snaps you off there — which is the stated justification for the mandatory two-speed reel, the locked drag and the 100–130 lb leader walked up from 60 lb ([snook — sliding sinker](../species/snook-sliding-sinker.md)). The open-bottom side: [species/snook.md](../species/snook.md#landing--handling) explains the boca's rise-not-dive strike read by reasoning that "there is nothing to rock a leader against in open water at the boca", contrasted against the mangrove roots. That sentence is a prior editor's mechanism for the strike read, not a quoted source claim, so the two are not equally weighted — but the KB currently states both. It matters to a planner: it decides whether this mark is fished on a structure build or a sand build. Flagged on the spot page created 2026-08-30 by the gazetteer pass; the router's sentence is out of a gazetteer unit's scope — **stands**: flag, never delete. Re-reading `EcQK5elTPJE` for an explicit bottom description, or any second source on the boca, settles it; a cross-note pass then carries the outcome to the router. The router's matching inline flag was added 2026-08-30 by the species/snook.md fact-check unit — one row, two inline locations |
| species/barracuda.md | wire versus no wire — the note's terminal doctrine rests on barracuda teeth cutting a monofilament leader "even in heavy sizes", so wire is the answer when barracuda are the target | contradicted-internal | `lm7D9Tlc7Po` | [bonito](../species/bonito.md) carries a Cameron-adjudicated ruling (2026-08-26, `⚠ adjudicated`) on the same question: barracuda and bonito both have sharp teeth, but "being bitten off by either is rare: no wire, and no reported case of a fish chewing through terminal gear" — a leader-class question, not a teeth question. That is the opposite premise to the one this note builds its Terminal line, its "Getting bit off" router row and half its Doctrine & conflicts on. `lm7D9Tlc7Po` is a general toothy-critter rigging video (wahoo, kingfish, barracuda), not a barracuda bite-off survey — **stands**: adjudicated wording is final and the fact-check pass never reconciles it. Flagged in place on the barracuda note; the two notes now visibly disagree, which is the honest state. Cameron extending the 2026-08-26 ruling to species/barracuda.md closes it |
| species/barracuda.md | Working a surface iron, the instruction is to wind faster, keeping the lure gliding on top, rod tip up | contradicted-internal | `ejUTAnONf7o` | [surface iron](../techniques/surface-iron.md) reads the SAME cite the opposite way on both variables: retrieve **tip-down**, because tip-up "pulls it up onto the surface where it stops swimming right" (`VpW91AKOFVQ`, `3dVc-2rsYII`), and the speed is "not a number: it is the slowest wind" that keeps the jig gliding, with the rod tip as the lever for lifting it *without* winding faster. The same note also records a flat denial that species-keyed retrieves exist — "a barracuda retrieve — no" (`Ec2GaNpiOHI`) — which the barracuda note's retrieve-speed conflict frame is built against without citing. The transcript supports the barracuda note's literal words ("keep that rod tip up", "keep going a little faster"), so this is two notes generalising one on-the-water coaching moment differently — **stands**: flagged on the barracuda note in both places (router row and Doctrine & conflicts). The cluster:barracuda unit, which already lists techniques/surface-iron.md as a member, is the right place to settle which reading is the general rule |

| species/barracuda.md | "Chrome is the barracuda colour — the one application worth throwing chrome for" | contradicted-by-source | `PrdPJy26H8c` | the cited line is "the only time I'll throw chrome is either for barracuda **or tuna**", so "the one application" drops half of its own source; the note's own evidence file quotes it correctly. | species/barracuda.md | small chrome jigs cover the same water when the fish are on small bait — the standing `unverifiable` flag's premise that "no corpus source ties barracuda jig size to bait size" | unverifiable, corroborated | `pcwcRdmWmLc` | the premise no longer holds. `pcwcRdmWmLc` ties barracuda jig SIZE to bait size explicitly (Tady AA-light / A1-light on rice anchovy, "two or three to one" against a full 45), and [surface iron](../techniques/surface-iron.md) already carries that downsize rule from the same source — which the barracuda note cites two sentences earlier in the same table cell. | species/barracuda.md | swapping the treble for "a single inline hook" lets about half the barracuda drop off without handling | contradicted-by-source | `kt3G72gUldM` | the source runs **two barbless** single inline hooks — a 3/0 BKK Lone Sniper in front and a 2/0 in the back — on a 120 mm stick bait, and it is the barbless hooks plus a deliberate shake of the rod that drop about half the fish ("shake the rod a little bit and have half of them come off"). The note reduces this to one hook and drops the barbless modification, which is the part actually doing the work; the "40 barracuda" figure it does not carry is stated on camera. | species/barracuda.md | on a sport boat the single inline hook is the standing choice "regardless of numbers" | contradicted-by-source | `xgPEt4Zj35Q` | the source states an unconditional personal preference for sport boats on safety and courtesy grounds and never mentions fish numbers at all, so "regardless of numbers" is the note's manufactured contrast with `28IttH7P7K0` (which switches to a single only "when there's a whole lot of barracuda"). The two sources are not actually addressing the same variable, which weakens the doctrine conflict the note builds from them. | species/barracuda.md | a barracuda on deck thrashes and "any hook not buried in its snout — an assist, the free points of a treble — ends up in someone's boots" | contradicted-by-source | `xgPEt4Zj35Q` | the source assigns the roles the other way round: the **treble** is the hook buried in the barracuda's snout and the **assist** hook is the one that "get[s] in everybody's boots" — "it's got the treble hook right in the barracuda's snout. They put that barracuda on the deck and now that thing's thrashing around and your assist hook's getting in everybody's boots." Free treble points in boots is not in the source. The generalisation is plausible and the safety conclusion is unchanged, but the note states a specific hazard geometry its source states differently — **stands**: flag, never delete |
| species/barracuda.md | choosing among jigs, the slower-swimming jig is the one that gets bit by barracuda and calico, where yellowtail want a faster-swimming jig | contradicted-by-source | `lV-UcWUvxgc` | doubly hedged in the source — "there **are times** where a slower presentation jig will get you bit, **say**, on a barracuda or a calico. The note hardens it into one half of a stated doctrine conflict, which gives a tendency the weight of a rule and sets it against `ejUTAnONf7o` as though the two were equally firm — **stands**: flag, never delete. Single-source ≠ wrong; the finding is the strength, not the direction |
| species/barracuda.md | Island variety trips built around live squid "produce" the three B's alongside yellowtail, white seabass and halibut | contradicted-by-source | `6-8KfjEg0x8` | the species list is that trip's stated pre-trip **goal**, not its production — "yellowtail, seabass, halibut are going to be the main goals, but there's a lot of bass, barracuda, bonito, the three B's around" — and none of the six was landed; the day's recap is whitefish, reds, blue bass, bocaccio and smaller lingcod. The source also says "seabass", not "white seabass". Presence ≠ catchability is a house rule, and this row states an aspiration as an outcome — **stands**: flag, never delete. The squid-day association is real and worth keeping; the verb is the finding |
| species/barracuda.md | fishing one large live mackerel screens barracuda, calico and bonito off the bait, "a yellowtail eats a 1½ lb mackerel without trouble, which is the size that screens the smaller fish out" | contradicted-by-source | `YVHdDbkQrKk`, `w5_x6kkN-xE` | the screening doctrine itself is verbatim in both transcripts, but the 1½ lb figure answers the separate question "how big a mackerel" as a yellowtail-capacity aside — "I've seen yellowtail eat one and a half pound mackerel, no problem" — and is never given as the threshold that screens the smaller fish out. The note fuses two answers into one parameter. The two cites are also a re-uploaded version of the same Your Saltwater Guide episode (near-identical start to finish), so the double cite reads as corroboration that does not exist; the evidence file already records the duplicate-of — **stands**: flag, never delete. Numerically precise and used as a router-row instruction, which is why it is flagged rather than left |

It is load-bearing twice over: it is the note's counter-example to the big-mackerel screen in Presence & forage and the reader-facing note on the flyline router row — **stands**: flag, never delete. An ambiguous antecedent is not a wrong claim, but the note states as barracuda-specific what the source leaves shared |
| species/barracuda.md | barracuda hold on the kelp lines, **boiler rocks** and beach edges of the coast and the islands | unverifiable | — | the beach edge is sourced (`DnSqw4r7A1s`, "guys fishing along the beach you're getting barracuda calicos and sand bass") and the kelp is sourced (`M6U_FVdosr4`, "short barracuda flopping around in the kelp"; `0KQ--N5TjqE`), but no corpus source puts barracuda on boiler rocks. The term is carried over from the calico-bass structure picture the note explicitly parallels, and it appears in both the lead and the first line of Where & when, where a new-to-SoCal reader would take it as a search instruction — **stands**: flag, never delete. Two of the three structure types are sourced; a corpus source putting barracuda on a boiler closes it |
| species/barracuda.md | jigs fished to roughly 80 ft on La Bocana rock structure drew a hookup "every single drop" | unverifiable | `qri15R3caYE` | the depth is verbatim ("about 80 feet down here") and the barracuda, the place and the inshore rock structure all check out, but the **quoted phrase** is not in the transcript: the auto-captions render it "every single draw" and "every single saw", never "every single drop". The sense is almost certainly right and the reconstruction is the obvious one, but the note presents as a direct quote a string the source does not contain, and the evidence file's asr-uncertain marker covers only the lure name — **stands**: flag, never delete. Low stakes for the doctrine, which rests on the sourced depth; the finding is the quotation marks. Re-captioning the clip settles it |
| species/barracuda.md | the no-wire-anywhere doctrine and "move the boat when toothy fish are thick enough to cost jigs", applied to SoCal barracuda | unverifiable | `-nIhadZwxAA` | both quotes are verbatim, but the seminar is Florida/Atlantic: its toothy-fish examples are kingfish, wahoo and sharks off Stuart, and the move-the-boat line names "barracudas and kingfish" together. CLAUDE.md requires out-of-region technique detail to be a labeled contrast block or excluded, never unlabeled beside SoCal doctrine, and this sits unlabeled in both a router row and Doctrine & conflicts on a note gated to `socal-bight`/`baja-pacific-*`. Atlantic great barracuda and California barracuda are not the same fish, which is the substantive half of the problem — **stands**: flag, never delete. Labeling it as a contrast block is a transform-pass edit; whether the doctrine transfers across the two barracuda species is Cameron's call |

| species/bonito.md | bonito "lack the barracuda's cutting teeth" so no wire is needed | contradicted-by-source | — | **resolved**: adjudicated by Cameron 2026-08-26: both species have sharp teeth and bite-offs are rare; the cost is baits and light line, so it is a leader-class question, not a wire question. The old premise was wrong and the conclusion right |
| techniques/dropper-loop.md | the double-dropper-loop build (rig format, double-hooking squid) and the "deadstick 1-3 rods soaking" default | cite-unresolved | `mUrihh0V59M` | **resolved**: the id was sitting unused in this note's own front-matter `sources` — `mUrihh0V59M` is "Dockside Tutorial: Dropper Loop Rig" (BDOutdoors, 2021-12-29), whose presenter self-IDs as "Dwayne Diego Malloy," an ASR mis-transcription of Capt. Duane Diego Mellor per `sources/source-registry.md`, which names this exact video as his dropper-loop entry. The transcript matches both claims closely (double dropper loop for rock cod/reds/chuckleheads/lingcod, straight braid to barrel swivel to fluoro to two octopus-offset hooks, double-hook squid for holding power against short-biting rockfish; one-to-two-even-three rods deadsticking in the holders while working a lead head or sliding-sinker rig). The leader-size/material and premade-P-Line-rig bullets in the same section are not in this transcript and stay sourced only via their linked tackle notes |
| locations/north-county-artificial-reefs.md | two distinct spot-library waypoints both labelled "Oceanside AR 2L" | contradicted-internal | cameron | **resolved**: resolved by geometry. Consecutive letters in the 2-series sit 0.09-0.16 nm apart; the second "2L" continues from the first at 0.161 nm on the same northward line, so it is a thirteenth module, not a second fix on one. It is not the missing 2D either - 2D belongs between 2C and 2E, 0.5 nm south. Both waypoints stand; only the label is open |
| locations/south-9-mile-bank-439.md | the Catalina Eddy shields the "inner San Diego banks" while it blows outside | contradicted-by-source | `S2L3KLSQ6Is` | **resolved**: adjudicated by Cameron 2026-08-26: the eddy shields everything east of Catalina and San Clemente. The prevailing wind down the California coast outside SoCal is from the north, so the islands shadow the water behind them and a mark well south (the 425) still sits in the lee. Rescoped on conditions/sea-state.md and this page |
| locations/south-9-mile-bank-439.md | the NW-event map — northern Channel Islands and the LA/Ventura shelf hit hardest, south of Newport less prone, Coronados/Catalina/San Clemente holding their water | contradicted-by-source | `HzE4FHHcvTk` | **resolved**: adjudicated by Cameron 2026-08-26: the map stands and will NOT be re-sourced to another video. To be backed by a per-zone SST rollover analysis across recorded NW events instead, which BightSST can answer. Flag rewritten as standing on both notes |
| locations/banda-bank-todos-santos-island.md | the coordinate census grouping Todos Santos Island with Banda Bank as one zone | contradicted-internal | cameron | **resolved**: queued for the relocation pass in sources/relocation-queue.md rather than regrouped inline - it re-parents spot pages and re-runs the census, so it is a structural change, not a note edit. The arithmetic in this row is the rationale on that queue row |
| locations/tanner-bank.md | the run from Long Beach out to Cortez Bank, stated in the corpus as both ~90 mi and 110 mi | contradicted-internal | `nQvJnfb5jQ4`, `c3NFkQbdDy0`, `fDSd9kqwYW0` | **resolved**: resolved by geometry, and it was never a disagreement. Tanner and Cortes lie 15.6 nm - 17.9 statute miles - apart, and the corpus names them interchangeably. A 20-mile spread across a complex 18 miles long is the near end against the far end: ~90 mi lands on this page's own Tanner row, 110 mi on Cortes. No figure was wrong and no chart was needed |
| locations/la-paz.md | the zone's only water temperature — 75 °F water under high-80s to mid-90s °F air | contradicted-internal | `92y14x33etQ` | **resolved**: resolved on weight of evidence. Three anchors put the reading in late October/November - the three uploads (2023-10-25 to 2023-11-09), the on-camera two-week stay ending that week, and every other dated day on the page. One misspoken or ASR-corrupted "springtime" does not outweigh three. Whether the number was recycled from an earlier trip stays open |
| seasonal/november-december.md | Tanner/Cortez bluefin "continue night-dominant with 300 lb fish through November" | contradicted-by-source | `Z3rZqy2Pi8E` | **resolved** (2026-08-28 transform, verifier pass): the 300 lb figure was legacy, uncited text carried since the note's first draft, matching no source in the note's own front-matter set. The note's own 11/23/22 report source instead gives 60–80 lb fish with a few 100+ lb mixed in, mostly on knife jigs or sinker rigs, with daytime bites mostly stopped by late November. Corrected to the cited, transcript-verified figure; the old 300 lb number is not carried forward anywhere (it was never sourced, so there is nothing to conserve into the evidence file) |
| fish-care/dorado-and-general.md | the dorado care doctrine (no bleed, ice immediately, deck-hose doesn't chill, never freshwater-rinse, car cooler for the drive home) | cite-unresolved | `E4vKwRaRueA` | **resolved** (2026-08-28 light transform): the note attributed this whole doctrine to `E4vKwRaRueA` ("Dorado Fishing Tactics"), but that transcript is entirely hooks/leader/kelp-paddy content and contains no fish-care material at all. The actual source is `5to3Q5P7w90` (BDOutdoors Bight report, 2022-09-14), which states every claim in a ~6-minute fish-care aside, including the salted-slurry recipe already used (and correctly cited) elsewhere in the KB. Re-cited to `5to3Q5P7w90`, appended to `sources` (append-only), `E4vKwRaRueA` left in place per the same rule. **Not yet fixed**: `species/dorado.md`'s Landing & handling section carries the identical claim under the same stale `E4vKwRaRueA` cite — out of scope for a fish-care-only transform, flagged there for a future pass. Separately, the note's "freshwater rinse dulls/washes out dorado's color" detail is not stated by `5to3Q5P7w90` or any other corpus source found; carried under an inline `⚠ Fact-check (unverifiable)` flag rather than dropped |
